# NCNN 部署指南（Ultralytics 官方文档归档）

> **系列定位**：本文是「部署格式专题」之一 —— 移动端 / 嵌入式通用推理框架（腾讯开源）。内容为 Ultralytics 官方英文文档完整归档，与中文实操文章互补。总目录见 [README 导读](README.md)。

> - 中文实操路线：[yolo模型推理与部署详解](YOLO模型推理与部署详解.md)

id="ultralytics-yolo-ncnn-export" class="group relative">Ultralytics YOLO NCNN Export<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#ultralytics-yolo-ncnn-export">#</a></h1>
<p>Deploying <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/computer-vision-cv" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">computer vision</a> models on devices with limited computational power, such as mobile or embedded systems, requires careful format selection. Using an optimized format ensures that even resource-constrained devices can handle advanced computer vision tasks efficiently.</p>
<p>Exporting to NCNN format allows you to optimize your <a href="https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Ultralytics YOLO26</a> models for lightweight device-based applications. This guide covers how to convert your models to NCNN format for improved performance on mobile and embedded devices.</p>
<h2 id="why-export-to-ncnn" class="group relative">Why Export to NCNN?<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#why-export-to-ncnn">#</a></h2>
<p align="center">
  <img alt="NCNN high-performance neural network inference framework" decoding="async" loading="lazy" src="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/ultralytics/assets@main/docs/ncnn-overview.avif" width="100%"/>
</p>
<p>The <a href="https://github.com/Tencent/ncnn" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">NCNN</a> framework, developed by Tencent, is a high-performance <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/neural-network-nn" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">neural network</a> inference computing framework optimized specifically for mobile platforms, including mobile phones, embedded devices, and IoT devices. NCNN is compatible with a wide range of platforms, including Linux, Android, iOS, and macOS.</p>
<p>NCNN is known for its fast processing speed on mobile CPUs and enables rapid deployment of <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/deep-learning-dl" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">deep learning</a> models to mobile platforms, making it an excellent choice for building AI-powered applications.</p>
<h2 id="key-features-of-ncnn-models" class="group relative">Key Features of NCNN Models<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#key-features-of-ncnn-models">#</a></h2>
<p>NCNN models provide several key features that enable on-device <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/machine-learning-ml" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">machine learning</a>, helping developers deploy models on mobile, embedded, and edge devices:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<p><strong>Efficient and High-Performance</strong>: NCNN models are lightweight and optimized for mobile and embedded devices like Raspberry Pi with limited resources, while maintaining high <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/accuracy" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">accuracy</a> on computer vision tasks.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Quantization</strong>: NCNN supports quantization, a technique that reduces the <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/precision" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">precision</a> of model weights and activations to improve performance and reduce memory footprint.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Compatibility</strong>: NCNN models are compatible with popular deep learning frameworks including <a href="https://www.tensorflow.org/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">TensorFlow</a>, <a href="https://caffe.berkeleyvision.org/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Caffe</a>, and <a href="https://onnx.ai/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">ONNX</a>, allowing developers to leverage existing models and workflows.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Ease of Use</strong>: NCNN provides user-friendly tools for converting models between formats, ensuring smooth interoperability across different development environments.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Vulkan GPU Acceleration</strong>: NCNN supports Vulkan for GPU-accelerated inference across multiple vendors including AMD, Intel, and other non-NVIDIA GPUs, enabling high-performance deployment on a wider range of hardware.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="deployment-options-with-ncnn" class="group relative">Deployment Options with NCNN<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#deployment-options-with-ncnn">#</a></h2>
<p>NCNN models are compatible with a variety of deployment platforms:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<p><strong>Mobile Deployment</strong>: Optimized for Android and iOS, enabling seamless integration into mobile applications for efficient on-device inference.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Embedded Systems and IoT Devices</strong>: Ideal for resource-constrained devices like Raspberry Pi and NVIDIA Jetson. If standard inference on a Raspberry Pi with the <a href="/guides/raspberry-pi">Ultralytics Guide</a> is insufficient, NCNN can provide significant performance improvements.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Desktop and Server Deployment</strong>: Supports deployment across Linux, Windows, and macOS for development, training, and evaluation workflows.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="vulkan-gpu-acceleration" class="group relative">Vulkan GPU Acceleration<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#vulkan-gpu-acceleration">#</a></h2>
<p>NCNN supports GPU acceleration through Vulkan, enabling high-performance inference on a wide range of GPUs including AMD, Intel, and other non-NVIDIA graphics cards. This is particularly useful for:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Cross-Vendor GPU Support</strong>: Unlike CUDA, which is limited to NVIDIA GPUs, Vulkan works across multiple GPU vendors.</li>
<li><strong>Multi-GPU Systems</strong>: Select a specific Vulkan device in systems with multiple GPUs using <code>device=&quot;vulkan:0&quot;</code>, <code>device=&quot;vulkan:1&quot;</code>, etc.</li>
<li><strong>Edge and Desktop Deployments</strong>: Leverage GPU acceleration on devices where CUDA is not available.</li>
</ul>
<p>To use Vulkan acceleration, specify the Vulkan device when running inference:</p><section class="admon admon-example"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-flask-conical admon-icon" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M14 2v6a2 2 0 0 0 .245.96l5.51 10.08A2 2 0 0 1 18 22H6a2 2 0 0 1-1.755-2.96l5.51-10.08A2 2 0 0 0 10 8V2"></path><path d="M6.453 15h11.094"></path><path d="M8.5 stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-4 w-4" aria-hidden="true"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

# Load the exported NCNN model

ncnn_model = YOLO(&quot;./yolo26n_ncnn_model&quot;)

# Run inference with Vulkan GPU acceleration (first Vulkan device)

results = ncnn_model(&quot;https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg&quot;, device=&quot;vulkan:0&quot;)

# Use second Vulkan device in multi-GPU systems

results = ncnn_model(&quot;https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg&quot;, device=&quot;vulkan:1&quot;)</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><section class="admon admon-success"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-lightbulb admon-icon" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M15 14c.2-1 .7-1.7 1.5-2.5 1-.9 1.5-2.2 1.5-3.5A6 6 0 0 0 6 8c0 1 .2 2.2 1.5 3.5.7.7 1.3 1.5 1.5 2.5"></path><path d="M9 18h6"></path><path d="M10 22h4"></path></svg><span>Vulkan Requirements</span></div><div class="admon-content"><p>Ensure you have Vulkan drivers installed for your GPU. Most modern GPU drivers include Vulkan support by default. You can verify Vulkan availability using tools like <code>vulkaninfo</code> on Linux or the Vulkan SDK on Windows.</p></div></section><h2 id="supported-tasks" class="group relative">Supported Tasks<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#supported-tasks">#</a></h2>
<p>NCNN export supports all seven Ultralytics tasks. Semantic segmentation and depth estimation are available only with YOLO26, the only family that ships those heads.</p>

<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Task</th><th style="text-align:left"><a href="/models/yolov8">YOLOv8</a></th><th style="text-align:left"><a href="/models/yolo11">YOLO11</a></th><th style="text-align:left"><a href="/models/yolo26">YOLO26</a></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/detect">Detect</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/segment">Segment</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/semantic">Semantic</a></td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/depth">Depth</a></td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/classify">Classify</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/pose">Pose</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/obb">OBB</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<h2 id="export-to-ncnn-converting-your-yolo26-model" class="group relative">Export to NCNN: Converting Your YOLO26 Model<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#export-to-ncnn-converting-your-yolo26-model">#</a></h2>
<p>You can expand model compatibility and deployment flexibility by converting YOLO26 models to NCNN format.</p>
<h3 id="installation" class="group relative">Installation<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#installation">#</a></h3>
<p>To install the required packages, run:</p><section class="admon admon-success"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" If you encounter any difficulties, consult our <a href="/guides/yolo-common-issues">Common Issues guide</a> for solutions.</p>
<h3 id="usage" class="group relative">Usage<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#usage">#</a></h3>
<p>All <a href="/models">Ultralytics YOLO26 models</a> are designed to support export out of the box, making it easy to integrate them into your preferred deployment workflow. You can <a href="/modes/export">view the full list of supported export formats and configuration options</a> to choose the best setup for your application.</p>
<p>The NCNN format supports the <a href="/modes/export">Export</a>, <a href="/modes/predict">Predict</a>, and <a href="/modes/val">Validate</a> modes. Export your model, then load the exported model to run inference or validate its accuracy.</p><section class="admon admon-example"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

# Load the exported NCNN model

model = YOLO(&quot;./yolo26n_ncnn_model&quot;)

# Validate accuracy on the COCO8 dataset

metrics = model.val(data=&quot;coco8.yaml&quot;)</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><h3 id="export-arguments" class="group relative">Export Arguments<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#export-arguments">#</a></h3>

<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th>Argument</th><th>Type</th><th>Default</th><th>Description</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>format</code></td><td><code>str</code></td><td><code>&#x27;ncnn&#x27;</code></td><td>Target format for the exported model, defining compatibility with various deployment environments.</td></tr><tr><td><code>imgsz</code></td><td><code>int</code> or <code>tuple</code></td><td><code>640</code></td><td>Desired image size for the model input. Can be an integer for square images or a tuple <code>(height, width)</code> for specific dimensions.</td></tr><tr><td><code>quantize</code></td><td><code>int</code> or <code>str</code></td><td><code>None</code></td><td>Quantization precision: <code>16</code> (FP16) reduces model size and can speed up inference; <code>32</code>/unset is FP32. Replaces the deprecated <code>half</code> flag.</td></tr><tr><td><code>batch</code></td><td><code>int</code></td><td><code>1</code></td><td>Specifies export model batch inference size or the max number of images the exported model will process concurrently in <code>predict</code> mode.</td></tr><tr><td><code>device</code></td><td><code>str</code></td><td><code>None</code></td><td>Specifies the device for exporting: GPU (<code>device=0</code>), CPU (<code>device=cpu</code>), MPS for Apple silicon (<code>device=mps</code>).</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p>For more details about the export process, visit the <a href="/modes/export">Ultralytics documentation page on exporting</a>.</p>
<h2 id="deploying-exported-yolo26-ncnn-models" class="group relative">Deploying Exported YOLO26 NCNN Models<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#deploying-exported-yolo26-ncnn-models">#</a></h2>
<p>After exporting your Ultralytics YOLO26 models to NCNN format, you can deploy them using the <code>YOLO(&quot;yolo26n_ncnn_model/&quot;)</code> method as shown in the usage example above. For platform-specific deployment instructions, see the following resources:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<p><strong><a href="https://github.com/Tencent/ncnn/wiki/how-to-build#build-for-android" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Android</a></strong>: Build and integrate NCNN models for <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/object-detection" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">object detection</a> in Android applications.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong><a href="https://github.com/Tencent/ncnn/wiki/how-to-build#build-for-macos" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">macOS</a></strong>: Deploy NCNN models on macOS systems.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong><a href="https://github.com/Tencent/ncnn/wiki/how-to-build#build-for-linux" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Linux</a></strong>: Deploy NCNN models on Linux devices including Raspberry Pi and similar embedded systems.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong><a href="https://github.com/Tencent/ncnn/wiki/how-to-build#build-for-windows-x64-using-visual-studio-community-2017" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Windows x64</a></strong>: Deploy NCNN models on Windows x64 using Visual Studio.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="summary" class="group relative">Summary<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#summary">#</a></h2>
<p>This guide covered exporting Ultralytics YOLO26 models to NCNN format for improved efficiency and speed on resource-constrained devices.</p>
<p>For additional details, refer to the <a href="https://ncnn.readthedocs.io/en/latest/index.html" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">official NCNN documentation</a>. For other export options, visit our <a href="/integrations">integration guide page</a>.</p>
<h2 id="faq" class="group relative">FAQ<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#faq">#</a></h2></div><script type="application/ld+json">{"@context":"https://schema.org","@type":"FAQPage","mainEntity":[{"@type":"Question","name":"How do I export Ultralytics YOLO26 models to NCNN format?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"To export your Ultralytics YOLO26 model to NCNN format: Python: Use the export method from the YOLO class. CLI: Use the yolo export command. For detailed export options, see the Export documentation."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"What are the advantages of exporting YOLO26 models to NCNN?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"Exporting your Ultralytics YOLO26 models to NCNN offers several benefits: Efficiency: NCNN models are optimized for mobile and embedded devices, ensuring high performance even with limited computational resources. Quantization: NCNN supports techniques like quantization that improve model speed and reduce memory usage. Broad Compatibility: You can deploy NCNN models on multiple platforms, including Android, iOS, Linux, and macOS. Vulkan GPU Acceleration: Leverage GPU acceleration on AMD, Intel, and other non-NVIDIA GPUs via Vulkan for faster inference. For more details, see the Why Export to NCNN? section."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"Why should I use NCNN for my mobile AI applications?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"NCNN, developed by Tencent, is specifically optimized for mobile platforms. Key reasons to use NCNN include: High Performance: Designed for efficient and fast processing on mobile CPUs. Cross-Platform: Compatible with popular frameworks such as TensorFlow and ONNX, making it easier to convert and deploy models across different platforms. Community Support: Active community support ensures continual improvements and updates. For more information, see the Key Features of NCNN Models section."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"What platforms are supported for NCNN model deployment?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"NCNN is versatile and supports various platforms: Mobile: Android, iOS. Embedded Systems and IoT Devices: Devices like Raspberry Pi and NVIDIA Jetson. Desktop and Servers: Linux, Windows, and macOS. For improved performance on Raspberry Pi, consider using NCNN format as detailed in our Raspberry Pi Guide."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"How can I deploy Ultralytics YOLO26 NCNN models on Android?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"To deploy your YOLO26 models on Android: Build for Android: Follow the NCNN Build for Android guide. Integrate with Your App: Use the NCNN Android SDK to integrate the exported model into your application for efficient on-device inference. For detailed instructions, see Deploying Exported YOLO26 NCNN Models. For more advanced guides and use cases, visit the Ultralytics deployment guide."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"How do I use Vulkan GPU acceleration with NCNN models?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"NCNN supports Vulkan for GPU acceleration on AMD, Intel, and other non-NVIDIA GPUs. To use Vulkan: For multi-GPU systems, specify the device index (e.g., vulkan:1 for the second GPU). Ensure Vulkan drivers are installed for your GPU. See the Vulkan GPU Acceleration section for more details."}}]}</script><section><div class="@container/main flex min-w-0 flex-1 flex-col md:p-6 mt-2 max-w-none gap-3 p-0"><ul class="grid gap-3"><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="how-do-i-export-ultralytics-yolo26-models-to-ncnn-format"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="how-do-i-export-ultralytics-yolo26-models-to-ncnn-format-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">How do I export Ultralytics YOLO26 models to NCNN format?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="how-do-i-export-ultralytics-yolo26-models-to-ncnn-format-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>To export your Ultralytics YOLO26 model to NCNN format:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<p><strong>Python</strong>: Use the <code>export</code> method from the YOLO class.</p>
<div class="code-block"><button type="button" tabindex="0" data-slot="tooltip-trigger" aria-label="Copy to clipboard" id="base-ui-_R_v8joqdd9inpampfivb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" class="group/button inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center border border-transparent bg-clip-padding text-sm font-medium whitespace-nowrap transition-all outline-none select-none focus-visible:border-ring focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 active:not-aria-[haspopup]:translate-y-px disabled:pointer-events-none disabled:opacity-50 aria-invalid:border-destructive aria-invalid:ring-3 aria-invalid:ring-destructive/20 dark:aria-invalid:border-destructive/50 dark:aria-invalid:ring-destructive/40 [&amp;_svg]:pointer-events-none [&amp;_svg]:shrink-0 [&amp;_svg:not([class*=&#x27;size-&#x27;])]:size-4 text-foreground hover:bg-muted hover:text-foreground aria-expanded:bg-muted aria-expanded:text-foreground dark:hover:bg-muted/50 size-7 rounded-[min(var(--radius-md),12px)] in-data-[slot=button-group]:rounded-lg code-copy"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-4 w-4" aria-hidden="true"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

# Load a YOLO26 model

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n.pt&quot;)

# Export to NCNN format

model.export(format=&quot;ncnn&quot;)  # creates &#x27;/yolo26n_ncnn_model&#x27;</code></pre></div>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>CLI</strong>: Use the <code>yolo export</code> command.</p>
<div class="code-block"><button type="button" tabindex="0" data-slot="tooltip-trigger" aria-label="Copy to clipboard" id="base-ui-_R_v93oqdd9inpampfivb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" class="group/button inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center border border-transparent bg-clip-padding text-sm font-medium whitespace-nowrap transition-all outline-none select-none focus-visible:border-ring focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 active:not-aria-[haspopup]:translate-y-px disabled:pointer-events-none disabled:opacity-50 aria-invalid:border-destructive aria-invalid:ring-3 aria-invalid:ring-destructive/20 dark:aria-invalid:border-destructive/50 dark:aria-invalid:ring-destructive/40 [&amp;_svg]:pointer-events-none [&amp;_svg]:shrink-0 [&amp;_svg:not([class*=&#x27;size-&#x27;])]:size-4 text-foreground hover:bg-muted hover:text-foreground aria-expanded:bg-muted aria-expanded:text-foreground dark:hover:bg-muted/50 size-7 rounded-[min(var(--radius-md),12px)] in-data-[slot=button-group]:rounded-lg code-copy"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-4 w-4" aria-hidden="true"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>yolo export model=yolo26n.pt format=ncnn # creates &#x27;/yolo26n_ncnn_model&#x27;</code></pre></div>
</li>
</ul>
<p>For detailed export options, see the <a href="/modes/export">Export</a> documentation.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="what-are-the-advantages-of-exporting-yolo26-models-to-ncnn"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="what-are-the-advantages-of-exporting-yolo26-models-to-ncnn-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">What are the advantages of exporting YOLO26 models to NCNN?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="what-are-the-advantages-of-exporting-yolo26-models-to-ncnn-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>Exporting your Ultralytics YOLO26 models to NCNN offers several benefits:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Efficiency</strong>: NCNN models are optimized for mobile and embedded devices, ensuring high performance even with limited computational resources.</li>
<li><strong>Quantization</strong>: NCNN supports techniques like quantization that improve model speed and reduce memory usage.</li>
<li><strong>Broad Compatibility</strong>: You can deploy NCNN models on multiple platforms, including Android, iOS, Linux, and macOS.</li>
<li><strong>Vulkan GPU Acceleration</strong>: Leverage GPU acceleration on AMD, Intel, and other non-NVIDIA GPUs via Vulkan for faster inference.</li>
</ul>
<p>For more details, see the <a href="#why-export-to-ncnn">Why Export to NCNN?</a> section.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="why-should-i-use-ncnn-for-my-mobile-ai-applications"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="why-should-i-use-ncnn-for-my-mobile-ai-applications-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">Why should I use NCNN for my mobile AI applications?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="why-should-i-use-ncnn-for-my-mobile-ai-applications-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>NCNN, developed by Tencent, is specifically optimized for mobile platforms. Key reasons to use NCNN include:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>High Performance</strong>: Designed for efficient and fast processing on mobile CPUs.</li>
<li><strong>Cross-Platform</strong>: Compatible with popular frameworks such as <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/tensorflow" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">TensorFlow</a> and ONNX, making it easier to convert and deploy models across different platforms.</li>
<li><strong>Community Support</strong>: Active community support ensures continual improvements and updates.</li>
</ul>
<p>For more information, see the <a href="#key-features-of-ncnn-models">Key Features of NCNN Models</a> section.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="what-platforms-are-supported-for-ncnn-model-deployment"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="what-platforms-are-supported-for-ncnn-model-deployment-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">What platforms are supported for NCNN model deployment?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="what-platforms-are-supported-for-ncnn-model-deployment-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>NCNN is versatile and supports various platforms:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Mobile</strong>: Android, iOS.</li>
<li><strong>Embedded Systems and IoT Devices</strong>: Devices like Raspberry Pi and NVIDIA Jetson.</li>
<li><strong>Desktop and Servers</strong>: Linux, Windows, and macOS.</li>
</ul>
<p>For improved performance on Raspberry Pi, consider using NCNN format as detailed in our <a href="/guides/raspberry-pi">Raspberry Pi Guide</a>.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="how-can-i-deploy-ultralytics-yolo26-ncnn-models-on-android"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="how-can-i-deploy-ultralytics-yolo26-ncnn-models-on-android-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">How can I deploy Ultralytics YOLO26 NCNN models on Android?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="how-can-i-deploy-ultralytics-yolo26-ncnn-models-on-android-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>To deploy your YOLO26 models on Android:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Build for Android</strong>: Follow the <a href="https://github.com/Tencent/ncnn/wiki/how-to-build#build-for-android" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">NCNN Build for Android</a> guide.</li>
<li><strong>Integrate with Your App</strong>: Use the NCNN Android SDK to integrate the exported model into your application for efficient on-device inference.</li>
</ol>
<p>For detailed instructions, see <a href="#deploying-exported-yolo26-ncnn-models">Deploying Exported YOLO26 NCNN Models</a>.</p>
<p>For more advanced guides and use cases, visit the <a href="/guides/model-deployment-options">Ultralytics deployment guide</a>.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="how-do-i-use-vulkan-gpu-acceleration-with-ncnn-models"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="how-do-i-use-vulkan-gpu-acceleration-with-ncnn-models-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">How do I use Vulkan GPU acceleration with NCNN models?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="how-do-i-use-vulkan-gpu-acceleration-with-ncnn-models-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>NCNN supports Vulkan for GPU acceleration on AMD, Intel, and other non-NVIDIA GPUs. To use Vulkan:</p>
<div class="code-block"><button type="button" tabindex="0" data-slot="tooltip-trigger" aria-label="Copy to clipboard" id="base-ui-_R_fjradd9inpampfivb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" class="group/button inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center border border-transparent bg-clip-padding text-sm font-medium whitespace-nowrap transition-all outline-none select-none focus-visible:border-ring focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 active:not-aria-[haspopup]:translate-y-px disabled:pointer-events-none disabled:opacity-50 aria-invalid:border-destructive aria-invalid:ring-3 aria-invalid:ring-destructive/20 dark:aria-invalid:border-destructive/50 dark:aria-invalid:ring-destructive/40 [&amp;_svg]:pointer-events-none [&amp;_svg]:shrink-0 [&amp;_svg:not([class*=&#x27;size-&#x27;])]:size-4 text-foreground hover:bg-muted hover:text-foreground aria-expanded:bg-muted aria-expanded:text-foreground dark:hover:bg-muted/50 size-7 rounded-[min(var(--radius-md),12px)] in-data-[slot=button-group]:rounded-lg code-copy"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-4 w-4" aria-hidden="true"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

# Load NCNN model and run with Vulkan GPU

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n_ncnn_model&quot;)
results = model(&quot;image.jpg&quot;, device=&quot;vulkan:0&quot;)  # Use first Vulkan device</code></pre></div>
<p>For multi-GPU systems, specify the device index (e.g., <code>vulkan:1</code> for the second GPU). Ensure Vulkan drivers are installed for your GPU. See the <a href="#vulkan-gpu-acceleration">Vulkan GPU Acceleration</a> section for more details.</p></div></div></div></li></ul></div></section><div class="mt-16 space-y-3 text-right text-sm Creators of YOLO.\\\"},{\\\"@type\\\":\\\"WebSite\\\",\\\"@id\\\":\\\"https://docs.ultralytics.com#website\\\",\\\"name\\\":\\\"Ultralytics Docs\\\",\\\"url\\\":\\\"https://docs.ultralytics.com\\\",\\\"publisher\\\":{\\\"@id\\\":\\\"https://www.ultralytics.com#organization\\\"}}]}\"}]\n30:T4f1,{\"@context\":\"https://schema.org\",\"@graph\":[{\"@type\":\"Article\",\"headline\":\"Ultralytics YOLO NCNN Export\",\"url\":\"https://docs.ultralytics.com/integrations/ncnn\",\"description\":\"Optimize YOLO26 models for mobile and embedded devices by exporting to NCNN format. Enhance performance in resource-constrained environments.\",\"image\":\"https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/ultralytics/assets@main/docs/ncnn-overview.avif\",\"datePublished\":\"2024-03-01T16:55:18+05:30\",\"dateModified\":\"2026-07-29T21:43:24+02:00\",\"author\":{\"@type\":\"Organization\",\"@id\":\"https://www.ultralytics.com#organization\",\"name\":\"Ultralytics\",\"url\":\"https://www.ultralytics.com\"},\"publisher\":{\"@type\":\"Organization\",\"@id\":\"https://www.ultralytics.com#organization\",\"name\":\"Ultralytics\",\"url\":\"https://www.ultralytics.com\",\"logo\":{\"@type\":\"ImageObject\",\"url\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/assets/main/logo/Ultralytics-logomark-color.png\"}}},{\"@type\":\"BreadcrumbList\",\"itemListElement\":[{\"@type\":\"ListItem\",\"position\":1,\"name\":\"Ultralytics Docs\",\"item\":\"https://docs.ultralytics.com/\"},{\"@type\":\"ListItem\",\"position\":2,\"name\":\"Integrations\",\"item\":\"https://docs.ultralytics.com/integrations\"},{\"@type\":\"ListItem\",\"position\":3,\"name\":\"NCNN\",\"item\":\"https://docs.ultralytics.com/integrations/ncnn\"}]}]}36:T3091,\n# Ultralytics YOLO NCNN Export\n\nDeploying [computer vision](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/computer-vision-cv) models on devices with limited computational power, such as mobile or embedded systems, requires careful format selection. Using an optimized format ensures that even resource-constrained devices can handle advanced computer vision tasks efficiently.\n\nExporting to NCNN format allows you to optimize your [Ultralytics YOLO26](https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics) models for lightweight device-based applications. This guide covers how to convert your models to NCNN format for improved performance on mobile and embedded devices.\n\n## Why Export to NCNN?\n\n\u003cp align=\"center\"\u003e\n  \u003cimg width=\"100%\" src=\"https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/ultralytics/assets@main/docs/ncnn-overview.avif\" alt=\"NCNN high-performance neural network inference framework\"\u003e\n\u003c/p\u003e\n\nThe [NCNN](https://github.com/Tencent/ncnn) framework, developed by Tencent, is a high-performance [neural network](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/neural-network-nn) inference computing framework optimized specifically for mobile platforms, including mobile phones, embedded devices, and IoT devices. NCNN is compatible with a wide range of platforms, including Linux, Android, iOS, and macOS.\n\nNCNN is known for its fast processing speed on mobile CPUs and enables rapid deployment of [deep learning](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/deep-learning-dl) models to mobile platforms, making it an excellent choice for building AI-powered applications.\n\n## Key Features of NCNN Models\n\nNCNN models provide several key features that enable on-device [machine learning](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/machine-learning-ml), helping developers deploy models on mobile, embedded, and edge devices:\n\n- **Efficient and High-Performance**: NCNN models are lightweight and optimized for mobile and embedded devices like Raspberry Pi with limited resources, while maintaining high [accuracy](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/accuracy) on computer vision tasks.\n\n- **Quantization**: NCNN supports quantization, a technique that reduces the [precision](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/precision) of model weights and activations to improve performance and reduce memory footprint.\n\n- **Compatibility**: NCNN models are compatible with popular deep learning frameworks including [TensorFlow](https://www.tensorflow.org/), [Caffe](https://caffe.berkeleyvision.org/), and [ONNX](https://onnx.ai/), allowing developers to leverage existing models and workflows.\n\n- **Ease of Use**: NCNN provides user-friendly tools for converting models between formats, ensuring smooth interoperability across different development environments.\n\n- **Vulkan GPU Acceleration**: NCNN supports Vulkan for GPU-accelerated inference across multiple vendors including AMD, Intel, and other non-NVIDIA GPUs, enabling high-performance deployment on a wider range of hardware.\n\n## Deployment Options with NCNN\n\nNCNN models are compatible with a variety of deployment platforms:\n\n- **Mobile Deployment**: Optimized for Android and iOS, enabling seamless integration into mobile applications for efficient on-device inference.\n\n- **Embedded Systems and IoT Devices**: Ideal for resource-constrained devices like Raspberry Pi and NVIDIA Jetson. If standard inference on a Raspberry Pi with the [Ultralytics Guide](../guides/raspberry-pi.md) is insufficient, NCNN can provide significant performance improvements.\n\n- **Desktop and Server Deployment**: Supports deployment across Linux, Windows, and macOS for development, training, and evaluation workflows.\n\n## Vulkan GPU Acceleration\n\nNCNN supports GPU acceleration through Vulkan, enabling high-performance inference on a wide range of GPUs including AMD, Intel, and other non-NVIDIA graphics cards. This is particularly useful for:\n\n- **Cross-Vendor GPU Support**: Unlike CUDA, which is limited to NVIDIA GPUs, Vulkan works across multiple GPU vendors.\n- **Multi-GPU Systems**: Select a specific Vulkan device in systems with multiple GPUs using `device=\"vulkan:0\"`, `device=\"vulkan:1\"`, etc.\n- **Edge and Desktop Deployments**: Leverage GPU acceleration on devices where CUDA is not available.\n\nTo use Vulkan acceleration, specify the Vulkan device when running inference:\n\n!!! example \"Vulkan Inference\"\n\n    === \"Python\"\n\n        ```python\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\n\n        # Load the exported NCNN model\n        ncnn_model = YOLO(\"./yolo26n_ncnn_model\")\n\n        # Run inference with Vulkan GPU acceleration (first Vulkan device)\n        results = ncnn_model(\"https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg\", device=\"vulkan:0\")\n\n        # Use second Vulkan device in multi-GPU systems\n        results = ncnn_model(\"https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg\", device=\"vulkan:1\")\n        ```\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Run inference with Vulkan GPU acceleration\n        yolo predict model='./yolo26n_ncnn_model' source='https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg' device=vulkan:0\n        ```\n\n!!! tip \"Vulkan Requirements\"\n\n    Ensure you have Vulkan drivers installed for your GPU. Most modern GPU drivers include Vulkan support by default. You can verify Vulkan availability using tools like `vulkaninfo` on Linux or the Vulkan SDK on Windows.\n\n## Supported Tasks\n\nNCNN export supports all seven Ultralytics tasks. Semantic segmentation and depth estimation are available only with YOLO26, the only family that ships those heads.\n\n| Task                             | [YOLOv8](../models/yolov8.md)                                                                                    | [YOLO11](../models/yolo11.md)                                                                                    | [YOLO26](../models/yolo26.md)                                                                                    |\n| :------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| [Detect](../tasks/detect.md)     | ✅     | ✅     | ✅     |\n| [Segment](../tasks/segment.md)   | ✅   | ✅   | ✅   |\n| [Semantic](../tasks/semantic.md) | ❌                                                                                                               | ❌                                                                                                               | ✅ |\n| [Depth](../tasks/depth.md)       | ❌                                                                                                               | ❌                                                                                                               | ✅       |\n| [Classify](../tasks/classify.md) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |\n| [Pose](../tasks/pose.md)         | ✅         | ✅         | ✅         |\n| [OBB](../tasks/obb.md)           | ✅           | ✅           | ✅           |\n\n\n## Export to NCNN: Converting Your YOLO26 Model\n\nYou can expand model compatibility and deployment flexibility by converting YOLO26 models to NCNN format.\n\n### Installation\n\nTo install the required packages, run:\n\n!!! tip \"Installation\"\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Install the required package for YOLO26\n        pip install ultralytics\n        ```\n\nFor detailed instructions and best practices, see the [Ultralytics Installation guide](../quickstart.md). If you encounter any difficulties, consult our [Common Issues guide](../guides/yolo-common-issues.md) for solutions.\n\n### Usage\n\nAll [Ultralytics YOLO26 models](../models/index.md) are designed to support export out of the box, making it easy to integrate them into your preferred deployment workflow. You can [view the full list of supported export formats and configuration options](../modes/export.md) to choose the best setup for your application.\n\nThe NCNN format supports the [Export](../modes/export.md), [Predict](../modes/predict.md), and [Validate](../modes/val.md) modes. Export your model, then load the exported model to run inference or validate its accuracy.\n\n!!! example \"Export\"\n\n    === \"Python\"\n\n        ```python\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\n\n        # Load a YOLO26 model\n        model = YOLO(\"yolo26n.pt\")\n\n        # Export the model to NCNN format\n        model.export(format=\"ncnn\")  # creates '/yolo26n_ncnn_model'\n        ```\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Export a YOLO26n PyTorch model to NCNN format\n        yolo export model=yolo26n.pt format=ncnn # creates '/yolo26n_ncnn_model'\n        ```\n\n!!! example \"Predict\"\n\n    === \"Python\"\n\n        ```python\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\n\n        # Load the exported NCNN model\n        model = YOLO(\"./yolo26n_ncnn_model\")\n\n        # Run inference\n        results = model(\"https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg\")\n        ```\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Run inference with the exported NCNN model\n        yolo predict model=./yolo26n_ncnn_model source='https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg'\n        ```\n\n!!! example \"Validate\"\n\n    === \"Python\"\n\n        ```python\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\n\n        # Load the exported NCNN model\n        model = YOLO(\"./yolo26n_ncnn_model\")\n\n        # Validate accuracy on the COCO8 dataset\n        metrics = model.val(data=\"coco8.yaml\")\n        ```\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Validate the exported NCNN model\n        yolo val model=./yolo26n_ncnn_model data=coco8.yaml\n        ```\n\n### Export Arguments\n\n| Argument   | Type             | Default  | Description                                                                                                                                 |\n| ---------- | ---------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `format`   | `str`            | `'ncnn'` | Target format for the exported model, defining compatibility with various deployment environments.                                          |\n| `imgsz`    | `int` or `tuple` | `640`    | Desired image size for the model input. Can be an integer for square images or a tuple `(height, width)` for specific dimensions.           |\n| `quantize` | `int` or `str`   | `None`   | Quantization precision: `16` (FP16) reduces model size and can speed up inference; `32`/unset is FP32. Replaces the deprecated `half` flag. |\n| `batch`    | `int`            | `1`      | Specifies export model batch inference size or the max number of images the exported model will process concurrently in `predict` mode.     |\n| `device`   | `str`            | `None`   | Specifies the device for exporting: GPU (`device=0`), CPU (`device=cpu`), MPS for Apple silicon (`device=mps`).                             |\n\nFor more details about the export process, visit the [Ultralytics documentation page on exporting](../modes/export.md).\n\n## Deploying Exported YOLO26 NCNN Models\n\nAfter exporting your Ultralytics YOLO26 models to NCNN format, you can deploy them using the `YOLO(\"yolo26n_ncnn_model/\")` method as shown in the usage example above. For platform-specific deployment instructions, see the following resources:\n\n- **[Android](https://github.com/Tencent/ncnn/wiki/how-to-build#build-for-android)**: Build and integrate NCNN models for [object detection](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/object-detection) in Android applications.\n\n- **[macOS](https://github.com/Tencent/ncnn/wiki/how-to-build#build-for-macos)**: Deploy NCNN models on macOS systems.\n\n- **[Linux](https://github.com/Tencent/ncnn/wiki/how-to-build#build-for-linux)**: Deploy NCNN models on Linux devices including Raspberry Pi and similar embedded systems.\n\n- **[Windows x64](https://github.com/Tencent/ncnn/wiki/how-to-build#build-for-windows-x64-using-visual-studio-community-2017)**: Deploy NCNN models on Windows x64 using Visual Studio.\n\n## Summary\n\nThis guide covered exporting Ultralytics YOLO26 models to NCNN format for improved efficiency and speed on resource-constrained devices.\n\nFor additional details, refer to the [official NCNN documentation](https://ncnn.readthedocs.io/en/latest/index.html). For other export options, visit our [integration guide page](index.md).\n\n## For more information, see the Key Features of NCNN Models section.\"}},{\"@type\":\"Question\",\"name\":\"What platforms are supported for NCNN model deployment?\",\"acceptedAnswer\":{\"@type\":\"Answer\",\"text\":\"NCNN is versatile and supports various platforms: Mobile: Android, iOS. do I export Ultralytics YOLO26 models to NCNN format?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/ncnn.md\",\"children\":\"To export your Ultralytics YOLO26 model to NCNN format:\\n\\n- **Python**: Use the `export` method from the YOLO class.\\n\\n    ```python\\n    from ultralytics import YOLO\\n\\n    # Load a YOLO26 model\\n    model = YOLO(\\\"yolo26n.pt\\\")\\n\\n    # Export to NCNN format\\n    model.export(format=\\\"ncnn\\\")  # creates '/yolo26n_ncnn_model'\\n    ```\\n\\n- **CLI**: Use the `yolo export` command.\\n\\n    ```bash\\n    yolo export model=yolo26n.pt format=ncnn # creates '/yolo26n_ncnn_model'\\n    ```\\n\\nFor detailed export options, see the [Export](../modes/export.md) documentation.\"}]}],[\"$\",\"$L54\",\"what-are-the-advantages-of-exporting-yolo26-models-to-ncnn\",{\"answerId\":\"what-are-the-advantages-of-exporting-yolo26-models-to-ncnn-answer\",\"id\":\"what-are-the-advantages-of-exporting-yolo26-models-to-ncnn\",\"question\":\"What are the advantages of exporting YOLO26 models to NCNN?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/ncnn.md\",\"children\":\"Exporting your Ultralytics YOLO26 models to NCNN offers several benefits:\\n\\n- **Efficiency**: NCNN models are optimized for mobile and embedded devices, ensuring high performance even with limited computational resources.\\n- **Quantization**: NCNN supports techniques like quantization that improve model speed and reduce memory usage.\\n- **Broad Compatibility**: You can deploy NCNN models on multiple platforms, including Android, iOS, Linux, and macOS.\\n- **Vulkan GPU Acceleration**: Leverage GPU acceleration on AMD, Intel, and other non-NVIDIA GPUs via Vulkan for faster inference.\\n\\nFor more details, see the [Why Export to NCNN?](#why-export-to-ncnn) section.\"}]}],[\"$\",\"$L54\",\"why-should-i-use-ncnn-for-my-mobile-ai-applications\",{\"answerId\":\"why-should-i-use-ncnn-for-my-mobile-ai-applications-answer\",\"id\":\"why-should-i-use-ncnn-for-my-mobile-ai-applications\",\"question\":\"Why should I use NCNN for my mobile AI applications?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/ncnn.md\",\"children\":\"NCNN, developed by Tencent, is specifically optimized for mobile platforms. Key reasons to use NCNN include:\\n\\n- **High Performance**: Designed for efficient and fast processing on mobile CPUs.\\n- **Cross-Platform**: Compatible with popular frameworks such as [TensorFlow](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/tensorflow) and ONNX, making it easier to convert and deploy models across different platforms.\\n- **Community Support**: Active community support ensures continual improvements and updates.\\n\\nFor more information, see the [Key Features of NCNN Models](#key-features-of-ncnn-models) section.\"}]}],[\"$\",\"$L54\",\"what-platforms-are-supported-for-ncnn-model-deployment\",{\"answerId\":\"what-platforms-are-supported-for-ncnn-model-deployment-answer\",\"id\":\"what-platforms-are-supported-for-ncnn-model-deployment\",\"question\":\"What with Your App**: Use the NCNN Android SDK to integrate the exported model into your application for efficient on-device inference.\\n\\nFor detailed instructions, see [Deploying Exported YOLO26 NCNN models?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/ncnn.md\",\"children\":\"NCNN supports Vulkan for GPU acceleration on AMD, Intel, and other non-NVIDIA GPUs. To use Vulkan:\\n\\n```python\\nfrom ultralytics import YOLO\\n\\n# Load NCNN model and run with Vulkan GPU\\nmodel = YOLO(\\\"yolo26n_ncnn_model\\\")\\nresults = model(\\\"image.jpg\\\", device=\\\"vulkan:0\\\")  # Use first Vulkan device\\n```\\n\\nFor multi-GPU systems, specify the device index (e.g., `vulkan:1` for the second GPU). Ensure Vulkan drivers are installed for your GPU.
